N_1 \ge x^{(D - 1)/D}$ and the rest is below $x^{H/D}$. The cells of the variable's primes are not read, and of the
rest only the lower ends and the sum of the upper ends, each with a margin of one unit. In (W$'$) the rest's members
are read in their cells, both ends, and the variable is tied to them: it is what they leave of $x$, within $2 \cdot
10^{-5}$ below and $10^{-5}$ above in exponents of $q$. In (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) the frozen
cofactor is always in the variable, with the chain's cofactor when it is not in the rest; the frozen part's variables
$a_2, \dots, a_k$ enter the tests through their cells when they are in the rest, and the range $(x^{e_-/D},
x^{e_+/D}]$ of $R$ only through $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$ and $c_*$. With the cofactor, (W) asks $h(\mathcal S_r) + 3
\le
l(\mathfrak b)$, so its rest leaves out at least one prime of the box.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:window-tests}
% Lean: (a) SieveAd.evI_resCEH, SieveAd.evI_resEH, SieveAd.evI_resYE, SieveAd.cof_facts, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_exp, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_cof, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_gen, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_cofG, SieveTypeIIHM.fullEst_of_tight; (b) SieveAd.evIF_froz_res, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resC, SieveAd.evIF_froz_gen, SieveAd.evIF_froz_genC, SieveAd.evIF_frozP_res, SieveAd.evIF_frozP_resC, SieveAd.frozCof_facts
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, and let $2 < L \le 5/2$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] The term of a box of $k \ge 1$ primes that passes (W) or (W$'$), with or without the cofactor, is
  evaluated; and it is so uniformly when the family is cut by at most $40 - 3k$ further comparisons, as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c): $q_0$ does not depend on the comparisons.
\item[(b)] Let $\mathcal M = \mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ be a frozen part of a box of $k \ge 2$ primes, with $0 \le e_-, e_+
  \le D$; in particular the wide and the narrow parts when $1 \le l_1 \le h_1 \le D$. If a chain $\mathfrak c$ of $j \ge
  1$ primes passes
  (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$), with or without the chain's cofactor, then $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak
  c) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

The proof of (a) is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(a), with Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest} or~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof} in
place of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}; the proof of (b) is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, with
Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest} in place of Lemma~\ref{lem:chains-two}. For (W$'$) the tuple estimate comes from
Lemma~\ref{lem:tied-to-tuples}. The formalisation states (a) with the kernel's tests below as hypotheses
(Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c)); (a) is the step inside the proofs of \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak evI\_resEH},
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak evI\_resCEH} and \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak evI\_resYE}, which use of the test only the
condition on the quadrant or the tied condition that it gives.

\begin{proof}
(a) The family $\mathcal F(\mathfrak b)$ is the set of the $k$-tuples of primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy $3k - 1$
comparisons, $2k$ for the cells and $k - 1$ for the order, with constant terms at most $L \log q$, and the further ones
bring the number to at most $40$. Take $e_0 = L/D$, since every prime is at least $x^{1/D}$, $J_0 = 40$ and $\Lambda_1 =
L + 1$. Every tuple of the family is strictly decreasing and has $x^{l_i/D} \le p_i < x^{h_i/D}$.

(W) without the cofactor. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest}(b) with the rest $\mathcal S_r$, $\rho_2 = Lh(\mathcal
S_r)/D$, $\varepsilon = L/D$, the lower bounds $\sigma_i = Ll_i/D$ for $i \in \mathcal S_r$ and $\rho_1 = Ll(\mathcal
S_r)/D$. Its hypotheses on the tuples hold: $p_i \ge q^{\sigma_i}$, and $q^{\rho_1} \le \prod_{\mathcal S_r} p_i \le
q^{\rho_2}$. Its condition on the quadrant, at $L - \rho_2 - \varepsilon = L(D - H - 1)/D$ and the lower ends
$\sigma_i - \varepsilon = L(l_i - 1)/D$, is (W), and $L - \rho_2 - \varepsilon > 0$ is $H + 1 < D$. Its bound
$q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$ is at most $\eta X$ for large $q$.

(W) with the cofactor. By $(\mathrm{Cf})$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(c), for large $q$ every tuple of the family
has $r =
p_1 \cdots p_k \le N_1$ and $N_2 < r p_k^2$, and every $y \ge p_k$ with $N_1 < ry \le N_2$ has $y \ge x^{l_*/D}$ and
$x^{(l(\mathcal S_r) + l_*)/D} \le y \prod_{\mathcal S_r} p_i \le x^{H/D}$, with $H = h(\mathcal S_r) + h_*$. These are
the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(b), with the rest $\mathcal S_r$ and the cofactor's lower bound
$Ll_*/D$,
$\rho_2 = LH/D$, $\varepsilon = L/D$ and $\rho_1 = L(l(\mathcal S_r) + l_*)/D$; its condition on the quadrant is (W)
with the cofactor, and it bounds $\Delta(\mathfrak b)$ by $q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$.

(W$'$). Lemma~\ref{lem:tied-to-tuples}, with the lower ends $Lc_j/D \ge 0$ and the upper ends $Ld_j/D$, turns (W$'$)
into the tuple estimate (Definition~\ref{def:tuple-estimate}) for the region $x^{c_j/D} \le z_j \le x^{d_j/D}$ of the
rest's entries, with $e_0 = L/D$. Without the cofactor, each prime of $\mathcal S_r$ lies in its cell, so in the region;
Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest}(a) gives the bound. With the cofactor, Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(c) puts every
admissible $y$ in $c_*$, which is the hypothesis of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(a).

The uniformity is that of Lemmas~\ref{lem:term-rest} and~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}: $\delta$ and $q_0$ depend only on
$J_0$, $\Lambda_1$, the exponents and the estimate, not on the comparisons, and the family with $J$ further
comparisons has $3k - 1 + J \le 40$ of them when $J \le 40 - 3k$, as $k \le 13$.

(b) As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, write the frozen cofactor, the integer $m$ of a pair $(\mathbf a,
m) \in \mathcal M$, as a non-increasing tuple $\mathbf y$ of $t$ primes at least $a_k$, and the chain's cofactor $k'$ as
a non-increasing tuple $\mathbf y'$ of $t'$ primes at
least $p'_j$; since every prime is at least $x^{1/D}$ and $n \le N_2 < x^{(D + 1)/D}$, $t, t' \le D$. Then
$\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c)$ is the sum over $t, t' \le D$ of the sums of $\vartheta(n)$ over the full tuples
$(\mathbf a, \mathbf y, \mathbf p', \mathbf y')$ of $(k - 1) + t + j + t'$ primes with product $n \in (N_1, N_2]$, cut
out by at most $3(k - 1) + 3j + 2 + (t^2 + t) + (t'^2 + t') + 2$ comparisons: the cells and the order of $\mathbf a$ and
of
$\mathbf p'$, the order of $\mathbf y$ and $y_i \ge a_k$, that of $\mathbf y'$ and $y'_i \ge p'_j$, the two of $R \in
(x^{e_-/D}, x^{e_+/D}]$ and the two of the range of $n$, with constant terms at most $(L + 1)\log q$. There is no order
between $\mathbf a$, $\mathbf y$ and $\mathbf p'$; Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest} asks none. Apply it to each
family, with $e_0 = L/D$, the rest at the positions of its variables among $\mathbf a$ and $\mathbf p'$, $\rho_2 =
Lh(\mathcal S)/D$ and $\rho_1 = Ll(\mathcal S)/D$ for the rest $\mathcal S$, $\varepsilon = L/D$ and the lower bounds
$Ll_v/D$: every entry is at least $x^{1/D}$, each member of the rest lies in its cell, and the rest's product lies in
$[x^{l(\mathcal S)/D}, x^{h(\mathcal S)/D}]$. Its condition is (W$_{\mathcal M}$), and it bounds each family by
$q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$, with $\delta$ and $q_0$ that depend on $t$, $t'$ and the fixed data only. The $(D + 1)^2$
families give $o(X)$.

With the chain's cofactor in the rest, $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$ gives, for large $q$ and every tuple $(\mathbf a,
\mathbf y, \mathbf p')$, $R p'_1 \cdots p'_j \le N_1$ and $N_2 < R p'_1 \cdots p'_j\, {p'_j}^2$, so only $t' = 1$
contributes, and its one prime $y'$ lies in $[x^{l_*/D}, x^{h_*/D}]$. It joins the rest as its last member, with the
lower bound $Ll_*/D$, $\rho_2 = L(h(\mathcal S) + h_*)/D$ and $\rho_1 = 0$, and the argument is the same. For
(W$'_{\mathcal M}$), Lemma~\ref{lem:tied-to-tuples} gives the tuple estimate for the region of the rest's cells, with or
without $c_*$, and it is applied to each family in place of Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest}: every member of the
rest lies in its cell, the chain's cofactor by $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$.
\end{proof}

The formalisation proves (b) for every frozen part with $e_\pm \le D$, with the conditions or with the kernel's tests
(below) as hypotheses; it does not prove (b) uniformly under further comparisons, and the trees over the frozen parts
do not need it. A node (E) of a tree
(Definition~\ref{def:tree}) and a node (E$_{\mathcal M}$) of a tree over a frozen part
(Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree}) may use these tests besides (I) and (I$_{\mathcal M}$), at the cost $0$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} holds for such trees with the same proof, the leaf cited from
Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(a); the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree}, the leaf cited from (b), holds for such trees
over a frozen part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_\pm \le D$ whose box $\mathfrak b'$ has cells with $l \ge 1$,
wherever it holds for the trees of Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak rules\_frozResYH}).
% Lean: SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.rules_frozResCH

\subsubsection*{What the kernel checks}

The conditions (W) and (W$'$) are statements about all real exponents and all classes of characters, and the kernel
cannot decide them. It decides two integer tests that imply them, each led by a hint that it does not trust. The
\emph{test of the resonance condition} $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L(c_1, \dots, c_K; H)$ (Lean
\lean{SieveResTest.\allowbreak resXH}) reads the rest's lower ends $c_j$ and the sum $H$ of its upper ends; the
\emph{test at the two corners} $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L((c_1, d_1), \dots, (c_K, d_K))$ (Lean
\lean{SieveResTest.\allowbreak resYH}) reads the rest's cells. The kernel's test of an evaluation by (W) or (W$'$), with
or without the cofactor, is the conjunction of the conditions on the box of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests} other than
the resonance condition, and of the first or the second test on the rest's cells (Lean \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak
chkIRCH}, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRY}; over a frozen part \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRFH},
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRFY}).

\emph{The test in mathematical terms.} Write $\sigma_j = L(c_j - 1)/D$ and $m_0 = L(D - H - 1)/D$. The exponents of a
class fit at $L$ (Definition~\ref{def:fit}) if, for its count exponent $\gamma$, its sizes $t_j$ and the bound
$\beta$ of the real parts of its zeros, at the exponents $m \ge m_0$ and $s_j \ge \sigma_j$ with $\sum_j s_j \le L$,
\[
  (\star) \qquad \sum_j s_j(t_j - 1) + \frac\gamma2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m) \le -\frac1{25000},
\]
with the clamp $\operatorname{cl}$ of Section~\ref{sec:corners}, the smaller of what orthogonality and the
Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality add for the variable (Lemma~\ref{lem:clamp}): when it equals its first entry,
$(\star)$ is the bound by orthogonality, and otherwise it gives both inequalities of the second alternative of
Definition~\ref{def:fit}, the diagonal and the off-diagonal term. It does
not increase in $m$, so the test takes it at $m = m_0$, and $(\star)$ must hold for every class that the
moments and the large values allow. For $\gamma \le 0$ the half-plane gives $t_j - 1 \le \beta + 4 \cdot 10^{-6} - 1 \le
-1/12 + 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$, and one inequality, with no hint, settles all of them. Since $g_{\mathrm H} \le 1$, $\gamma
\le 1$. The hint cuts $(0, 1]$ into at most $15$ intervals with ends on the grid $2^{-12}$. On an interval $[a, b]$ each
member of the rest gets an upper line in $\gamma$ for $s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau$: the half-plane and the curve, through
the bound of $\beta$ that the curve's pieces give for $\gamma \ge a$; the moments $\mathfrak m_{k_0 - 1}$ and
$\mathfrak m_{k_0}$, $k_0 = \lceil 1/\sigma_j \rceil$ for $\sigma_j > 0$ (the kernel takes $\sigma_j$ rounded down to
the fixed point, \S S7); or the large values $\mathfrak v_{k_0 - 1}$ and $\mathfrak v_{k_0 - 2}$, where
they apply. The term $\operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m_0)$ gets one of three upper lines. The sum of the chosen lines, with
$\gamma/2$,
$L\tau$ and $1/25000$, must be at most $0$ at $\gamma = a$ and at $\gamma = b$. It is affine in $\gamma$ but for the
large values' terms, which are convex and are replaced by their chords, so the two ends suffice. Everything is computed
in fixed point with the unit $2^{-64}$, every rounding in the direction that keeps the computed sum above the true one.
The test at the two corners runs this test twice, at the upper corner of the rest's cells with a shortened variable and
at the lower corner with a variable where the clamp is $0$; Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners} is why that suffices. The
details are in \S S7.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:window-kernel}
% Lean: SieveResTest.resXH_sound_cells, SieveResTest.resXH_hs, SieveResTest.resY_sound, SieveResTest.resY_room, SieveResTest.resYH_eq; the tests of the search: SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.evI_resCEH, SieveAd.evI_resYE; over frozen parts: SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTCH, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTY, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH
Let $L = L_1/L_2$ with integers $L_1 \ge 0$, $L_2 \ge 1$. Whatever the hint:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] if $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L(c_1, \dots, c_K; H)$ passes, with integers $c_j \ge 1$ and $H \ge 0$,
  then $H + 1 < D$ and the condition on the quadrant of (W) holds;
\item[(b)] if $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L((c_1, d_1), \dots, (c_K, d_K))$ passes, then $c_j \le d_j$ for every
  $j$, $\sum_j Ld_j/D + 2 \cdot 10^{-5} \le L$, and the tied condition of (W$'$) holds;
\item[(c)] under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, for $2 < L \le 5/2$, a box of $k \le 13$ primes that
passes the kernel's test of an evaluation by (W) or
  (W$'$), with or without the cofactor, has its term evaluated, uniformly as in Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(a); and a
  chain of $j \ge 1$ primes that passes the kernel's test of (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) over a frozen
  part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_\pm \le D$ has $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) and (b) are proved in \S S7: (a) by the two cases in $\gamma$ above, and (b) by (a)'s argument at the two corners
and Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners}. A passing test also has $c_j \ge 1$ for the rest's lower ends, as every cell has $l
\ge 1$ and $l_* \ge 1$. So the kernel's test of an evaluation implies the conditions of
Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}; (b) gives the room $2 \cdot 10^{-5}$, more than the $10^{-5}$ that (W$'$) asks.
Then (c) is Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}.
\end{proof}

At $21/10$ the search's tests (Lean \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak resTestsFYH}) are the test at the two corners for an
evaluation by (W$'$), the test of the window with its lines named by the hint for one by (W) (the library's
configurations of kind $4$ and of kind $3$, \S S7), and the tests (I), (J2)
and (J3) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} otherwise, those at a floor with the leaves' condition (L) or
(L$_3$) (Definition~\ref{def:leaves3}); over the frozen parts, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak frozResTestsYH}, the
same with the tests of Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree}. The formalisation proves that every box that passes them at a
configuration has its term, or its terms at a floor, evaluated (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak resTestsFYH\_sound}), and that
an evaluation by (W) or (W$'$) in a tree over a frozen part is a leaf of cost $0$ (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak
rules\_frozResYH}). So, under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl} and for $2 < L \le 5/2$, the search with
these tests is sound
whatever its hints, as Lemma~\ref{lem:search} is for the search of Section~\ref{sec:certificate};
Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b}(a) states it.

\section{Two more rules}\label{sec:rules}

Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} is proved by the decomposition of Section~\ref{sec:decomp}, with the tests of the window of
Section~\ref{sec:window} and two more rules. The first concerns the leaves of a comparison. The tests (J2) and (J3) of
Definition~\ref{def:tests} evaluate the leaves by Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}, and for that they ask the leaves' condition
(L). Its second inequality, $a_1 + a_2 < 1 - 1/(2L)$, says that the cofactor of a leaf, the integer divided by $d_1
d_2$, is longer than $x^{1/(2L)} = q^{1/2}$, and for $d_1 \ge q$ the rule of Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} says the same. So
the tests do not evaluate the leaves whose cofactor is shorter. Such a leaf is a sum over the integers $n$ divisible by
$d_1 d_2$ whose cofactor $n/(d_1 d_2)$ is $w$-rough, and the first rule evaluates it by an estimate in two variables in
which $d_1$ is the first variable, the cofactor the second, and the primes of $d_2$ the rest. A variable shorter than
$\sqrt q$ needs
a third bound for its mean square over a bin, by a fourth moment: Section~\ref{sec:leaf-estimate} gives it, and the
estimate on a region of the leaves. Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three} cuts the leaves of a comparison in three parts, two
that Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} reaches and a third in that region, and gives the condition (L$_3$) that the tests may ask
in place of (L). The second rule is the reversal of a term of the sign $+$. Section~\ref{sec:reversal} reverses a box
of the sign $-$; a box of the sign $+$ that no test evaluates is dropped or decomposed there.
Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus} reverses it, with the roles of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ exchanged.

\subsection{The estimate in two variables with a fourth moment}\label{sec:leaf-estimate}

Let $W(\chi) = \sum_{m \in S} c_m \chi(m) m^{-ih_0}$ with $S \subseteq (P, 2P]$ and $V = \sum_{m \in S} |c_m|^2$, as
in Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds}. For $P$ below $q$ orthogonality bounds the mean square over a bin by about $qV$
whatever the bin; for $P$ below $\sqrt q$ the square $W(\chi)^2$ is a polynomial of length about $P^2 \le q$, and
orthogonality applied to it gives a better bound in the bins with few characters.

\begin{lemma}[the fourth moment over the characters]\label{lem:fourth}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.sum_chars_dp_four_le, SieveTypeIIHM.sum_bin_four_le, SieveTypeIIHM.card_fiber_le_pow
Let $q \ge 1$ and $P \ge 0$ be integers, $S \subseteq (P, 2P]$, $c_m \in \mathbb C$ and $h_0 \in \mathbb R$, and let
$F$ be a real number such that for every $n$ at most $F$ pairs $(j, k) \in S \times S$ have $jk = n$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $\sum_\chi |W(\chi)|^4 \le (3P^2 + q) F V^2$, summed over all the characters mod $q$.
\item[(b)] For every set $\mathcal X$ of characters mod $q$, $\sum_{\chi \in \mathcal X} |W(\chi)|^2 \le |\mathcal
  X|^{1/2} \bigl((3P^2 + q) F\bigr)^{1/2} V$.
\item[(c)] For $0 < \varepsilon \le 1$ there is $C_\varepsilon \ge 1$, depending only on $\varepsilon$, with $d(n) \le
  C_\varepsilon n^\varepsilon$ for every $n \ge 1$; for it, $F = C_\varepsilon (4P^2)^\varepsilon$ bounds the number of
  pairs as above.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof applies the first bound of Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds} to $W(\chi)^2$. Since $0 \notin S$, $\chi(jk) =
\chi(j)\chi(k)$ and $(jk)^{-ih_0} = j^{-ih_0} k^{-ih_0}$ give $W(\chi)^2 = \sum_n c'_n \chi(n) n^{-ih_0}$, summed over
the products $n = jk$ of two elements of $S$, with $c'_n = \sum_{jk = n} c_j c_k$. These products lie in $(P^2, 4P^2]$,
an interval of length $3P^2$. The first bound of Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds} holds for a polynomial over any interval
$(A, A + M]$ with $M + q$ in place of $P + q$, with the same proof, since a residue class mod $q$ has at most $M/q + 1$
elements there. So $\sum_\chi |W(\chi)|^4 = \sum_\chi |W(\chi)^2|^2 \le (3P^2 + q) \sum_n |c'_n|^2$. By Cauchy--Schwarz
over the at most $F$ pairs with $jk = n$, $|c'_n|^2 \le F \sum_{jk = n} |c_j|^2 |c_k|^2$, and the sum of the right side
over $n$ is $F V^2$. This is (a). For (b), Cauchy--Schwarz over $\mathcal X$ gives $\sum_{\mathcal X} |W|^2 \le
|\mathcal
X|^{1/2} (\sum_{\mathcal X} |W|^4)^{1/2}$, and the sum over $\mathcal X$ is at most the sum over all the characters. For
(c), the map $(j, k) \mapsto j$ sends the pairs with $jk = n$ injectively to divisors of $n$, and $n \le 4P^2$ if there
is such a pair.
\end{proof}

Part (a) is orthogonality applied to the square of the polynomial, with a divisor bound for the coefficients. It is not
a fourth moment of $L$-functions: the hybrid fourth moment of $L(1/2 + it, \chi)$ is a different result, which the
density estimates behind the curves use. In exponents, for a polynomial of length $q^m$ over a bin of about
$q^{g(\beta)}$ characters and with $F \le C q^{2\varepsilon'}$, (b) gives the exponent $g(\beta)/2 + \max(m, 1/2) +
\varepsilon'$ of the mean square, which Definition~\ref{def:zone4} rounds up to $g(\beta)/2 + \max(m, 1/2) +
2\varepsilon'$. For $m \le 1/2$ orthogonality loses the factor $q^{1 - m}$ against $q^m V$, whatever the bin, and the
third bound the factor $q^{g(\beta)/2 + 1/2 - m + 2\varepsilon'}$: it does better as soon as $g(\beta) < 1 -
4\varepsilon' = 0.996$, but not in a bin counted trivially.

\begin{definition}\label{def:zone4}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.SqExp4, SieveTypeIIHM.twoBound4, SieveTypeIIHM.ZoneOK4
In the setting of Section~\ref{sec:zone}, a number $M$ is an \emph{exponent with the third bound} of the mean square of
a polynomial of length $q^m$ over the bin if it is an exponent there, or
\[
  M \ge \max\Bigl(m,\ \frac{g(\beta)}2 + \max\Bigl(m, \frac12\Bigr) + 2\varepsilon'\Bigr).
\]
A set $\mathcal R$ of pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ satisfies the \emph{zone condition with the third bound} for a curve $g$ at
$L$ if for every $1/2 \le \beta \le 11/12$, every $0 \le \varpi \le 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$ and every $(m_1, m_2) \in
\mathcal R$ there are exponents with the third bound $M_1$ and $M_2$ of the two mean squares, with $\delta' =
\varepsilon' = 1/1000$, such that $E \le L - 1/25000$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[the estimate with the third bound]\label{prop:type2-gen4}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.typeII_two_gen4 (with SieveTypeIIHM.bin_bound_two4, SieveTypeIIHM.sqTerm4_le_pow, SieveTypeIIHM.sum_typeII_two_bins4)
Assume Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}. Let $g$ be a density curve with $g \le 1$ and $g(1/2) = 1$, and let $\mathcal R$
satisfy the zone condition with the third bound for $g$ at $L$. Then the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds
with $\mathcal R$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen}, with one more case in Lemma~\ref{lem:bin} and a bound for the
pairs. Suppose, in Lemma~\ref{lem:bin}, that in addition $0 \le F_i \le C_F
q^{2\varepsilon'}$ bounds for every $n$ the number of pairs $(j, k) \in S_i \times S_i$ with $jk = n$. Then the lemma
holds for a set that satisfies the zone condition with the third bound, with $C' = C_0(e(2 + 12C_dC_K) +
(12C_dC_F)^{1/2})$. The new case is an exponent $M_i$ by the third bound. Since $(qT)^{g(\beta)} \le 3q^{g(\beta) +
\eta}$ and $3P_i^2 + q \le 4q^{2\max(m_i, 1/2)}$, Lemma~\ref{lem:fourth}(b) bounds the mean square by
\begin{multline*}
  (3C_d)^{1/2} (1 + \log qT)^5 q^{(g(\beta) + \eta)/2} \cdot 2C_F^{1/2} q^{\max(m_i, 1/2) + \varepsilon'} V_i \\
  \le (12C_dC_F)^{1/2} (1 + \log qT)^{10} q^{M_i + \eta} V_i,
\end{multline*}
since $M_i \ge g(\beta)/2 + \max(m_i, 1/2) + 2\varepsilon'$, and the remainder of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:bin} is
unchanged. In the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen}, take $\varepsilon = 1/(1000(|L| + 2))$ in
Lemma~\ref{lem:fourth}(c). For $q \ge 2^{K_0}$, since every $Q_j \ge 1$, $P_1P_2 \le 2^{K_0} q^L \le q^{1 + L}$, so $0
\le m_i \le 1 + L$ and $C_\varepsilon (4P_i^2)^\varepsilon \le 4C_\varepsilon q^{2m_i\varepsilon} \le 4C_\varepsilon
q^{2/1000}$: this is $F_i \le C_F q^{2\varepsilon'}$ with $C_F = 4C_\varepsilon$. The remainder of the proof is
unchanged.
\end{proof}

\emph{The leaves' region.} At a leaf, $d_1 = q^{m_1}$ is the first variable, the cofactor $q^{m_2}$ the second, and
$d_2 = q^\rho$ the rest, $\rho = L - m_1 - m_2$. The region below is a box cut by seven inequalities, each a case of the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:leaf-zone}.

\begin{definition}\label{def:leaf-region}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.LeafZone, SieveLeafTwo.LeafZoneX
Let $L$ be real. The set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ is the set of the pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ of reals such that, with
$\rho = L - m_1 - m_2$,
\[
  \frac9{10} \le m_1 \le \frac65, \qquad \frac25 \le m_2 \le \frac12, \qquad \frac3{10} \le \rho \le 1,
\]
and
\begin{align*}
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{749993}{1750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{25001}{25000}, \tag*{(C1)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{749993}{1750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{12501}{25000}, \tag*{(C2)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{1174979}{5250000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{73693427}{84000000}, \tag*{(C3)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{62497}{750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{87937}{300000}, \tag*{(C4)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{748427}{2625000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{10274479}{11200000}, \tag*{(C5)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{748427}{2625000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{4674479}{11200000}, \tag*{(C6)} \\
  \tfrac{2753}{6000} m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{1174979}{5250000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{70467547}{84000000}. \tag*{(C7)}
\end{align*}
The \emph{leaves' region} $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ is the set of the pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ with either $m_2 \le 1/2$
and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$, or $m_2 \ge 1/2$ and $(m_1, 1/2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L -
m_2 + 1/2}$.
\end{definition}

In the second case the rest is the same, $(L - m_2 + 1/2) - m_1 - 1/2 = \rho$: a cofactor longer than $\sqrt q$ is in
the region when the point with the same $m_1$ and $\rho$ and the cofactor $\sqrt q$ is. The coefficients of $\rho$ are
$1 - \beta - 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$ at $\beta = 4/7$, $163/210$, $11/12$ and $1201/1680$, and $2753/6000 = 1/2 - (1/12 -
\delta')/2$. Each cut is the case $(m_1 - c_1)/2 + (m_2 - c_2)/2 + \rho(1 - \beta - 4 \cdot 10^{-6}) \ge 1/25000$ of $E
\le L - 1/25000$ at $\varpi = 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$, for the point $\beta$ and the case with $c_i$ of the table below in
place of $M_i$ (a computation, \S S8), with $g^*(\beta) = A(1 - \beta)$ for the exponent $A$ of the piece of $g^*$ used
there. The pieces of (C3), (C5), (C6) and (C7) are steps that Input~\ref{inp:cgl} gives; (C3) is at the right end of its
piece, (C7) at the left end of the next.

\begin{center}
\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllll@{}}
\toprule
cut & $\beta$ & $A$ & $c_1$ ($d_1$) & $c_2$ (cofactor) \\
\midrule
(C1) & $4/7$ & ($g^* = 1$) & $1$ & $1$ \\
(C2) & $4/7$ & ($g^* = 1$) & $m_1$ & $1$ \\
(C3) & $163/210$ & $225661/100000$ & $1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C4) & $11/12$ & $101/50$ & $m_1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C5) & $1201/1680$ & $11667/5000$ & $1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C6) & $1201/1680$ & $11667/5000$ & $m_1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C7) & $163/210$ & $225207/100000$ & $A(1 - \beta) + b(m_1)$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:leaf-zone}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.zone_leaf, SieveLeafTwo.zone_leafX (with SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_OO, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_OE, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_HE, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_of_ends, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_glue, SieveLeafTwo.sqExp4_shift, SieveLeafTwo.twoBound4_shift)
For every real $L$, the sets $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ and $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ satisfy the zone condition
with the third bound for $g^*$ at $L$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
With $\Delta_i = M_i - m_i$, the inequality $E \le L - 1/25000$ reads $\Delta_1 + \Delta_2 + \mu \le 2\rho(1 - \beta -
\varpi)$, as in Section~\ref{sec:region}. On $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$, $\rho \ge 3/10 \ge 0$, so every case below
increases with $\varpi$: take $\varpi = 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$. The first variable takes orthogonality, $\Delta_1 = \max(0, 1
- m_1)$, or the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality, $\Delta_1 = \max(0, g^*(\beta) - T_* + H_1(1 - m_1))$. The cofactor,
of length at most $\sqrt q$, takes orthogonality, $\Delta_2 = 1 - m_2$, or the third bound, where $\max(m_2, 1/2) = 1/2$
and $\Delta_2 = \max(0, g^*(\beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon' - m_2)$.

\emph{For $1/2 \le \beta \le 4/7$} ($g^* = 1$ below $4/7$), take both exponents by orthogonality, whatever the curve.
Each of the four choices of the maxima is affine in $\beta$. \emph{On each of the $49$ pieces $[\beta_-, \beta_+]$ of
$g^*$ from $4/7$ to $163/210$}, where $g^*(\beta) = A(1 - \beta)$, take $d_1$ by orthogonality and the cofactor by the
third bound. \emph{On each of the $17$ pieces from $163/210$ to $11/12$}, the last one used on $[5/6, 11/12]$, take
$d_1$ by the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality and the cofactor by the third bound. The pieces of the second case are
those with $A(1 - \beta_+) \ge T_* = 249/500$, the value of the curve below which the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality
does better for a variable of length $q$ (Section~\ref{sec:region}): the last of them has $A(1 - \beta_+) =
0.50505\ldots$ at $\beta_+ = 163/210$, and the first piece of the third case has $0.49599\ldots$ at $\beta_+ =
131/168$. On every
piece the exponents are affine in $\beta$, and so is each of the four choices of the maxima; so it suffices to check the
four cases at the two ends of each piece. A piece of $g^*$ is $[\beta_-, \beta_+)$; the case at $\beta_+$ is checked
with that piece's $A$, and the next piece covers $\beta_+$ itself.

At an end of a piece, a case is a linear inequality in $(m_1, m_2, \rho)$, and $L$ does not enter it. The inequalities
of Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region} define a polytope in $(m_1, m_2, \rho)$, bounded by the box and independent of $L$.
The $67 \times 2 \times 4 = 536$ cases hold at its vertices, in exact rational arithmetic (\S S8), and so on the
polytope; the seven cuts are seven of them, with no slack. In the formalisation each case
is a linear combination of the $13$ inequalities with nonnegative coefficients, for every $L$.

For $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ it remains to take $m_2 \ge 1/2$, with $(m_1, 1/2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L'}$
and $L' = L - m_2 + 1/2$. The first part gives exponents $M_1$ and $M_2$ at $(\beta, \varpi)$ for $(m_1, 1/2)$ at
$L'$. Then $M_1$ and $M_2 + (m_2 - 1/2)$ are exponents with the third bound for $(m_1, m_2)$. Indeed $M_2 \ge 1/2$,
so $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge m_2$; and, with $g = g^*(\beta)$, if $M_2 \ge 1$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge 1$; if $M_2 \ge g +
b(1/2)$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge g + b(m_2)$, since $b(m_2) - b(1/2) = (m_2 - 1/2)(1/12 - \delta') \le m_2 - 1/2$; and
if $M_2 \ge g/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge g/2 + \max(m_2, 1/2) + 2\varepsilon'$. The rest $\rho$
is the same, and both sides of $E \le L - 1/25000$ grow by $m_2 - 1/2$ from $L'$ to $L$.
\end{proof}

At $L = 21/10$ the set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ is the quadrilateral with the vertices $(m_1, m_2, \rho) \approx
(0.98276, 0.5, 0.61724)$, $(0.98677, 0.49599, 0.61724)$, $(1.00091, 0.48396, 0.61513)$ and $(1.01299, 0.5, 0.58701)$,
cut out by $m_2 \le 1/2$, (C5), (C6) and (C7) (a computation, \S S8). In exponents of $x$, $d_1$ lies in $[x^{0.468},
x^{0.4824}]$, the cofactor in $[x^{0.2305}, x^{0.2381}]$ and $d_2$ in $[x^{0.2795}, x^{0.2939}]$. For $m_2 \ge 1/2$ the
condition on $(m_1, \rho)$ is a polygon that does not depend on $L$.

\emph{Why the curve is $g^*$.} At a $\beta$ where the curve is $1$, the cofactor costs at least $1 - m_2 \ge 1/2$: the
third bound costs $1 + 2\varepsilon' - m_2$, and the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality more. So the zone condition asks
$1/2 + \mu \le 2\rho(1 - \beta)$ there. With $g_{\mathrm H}$, which counts trivially up to $\beta^\dagger = 0.7789$,
that is about $\rho \ge 1.1309$, a rest longer than $q$, while $\rho \le 0.6173$ on $\mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{21/10}$. With $g^*$, trivial only below $4/7$, it is about $\rho \ge 0.5834$, the cut (C2) at $m_2 =
1/2$. With the trivial count up to $5/7$ it would be about $\rho \ge 0.8751$ (computations, not formalised). So the
leaves' region needs a density estimate below $5/7$; it uses Ingham's from $4/7$ and those of
Input~\ref{inp:cgl} from $5/7$, at whose pieces the cuts (C3), (C5), (C6) and (C7) lie. The curve $g^*$ does not replace
$g_{\mathrm H}$ elsewhere: on $[7789/10000, 131/168)$ it is the larger of the two, and the estimate on $\mathcal R_L$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} keeps $g_{\mathrm H}$.

\begin{theorem}[the estimate on the leaves' region]\label{thm:type2-leaves}
% Lean: SieveLeafTwo.typeIITwoAt_leafX (from SieveTypeIIHM.typeII_two_gen4, SieveLeafTwo.zone_leafX, SieveCurve7D.counts_7d, SieveCurve7D.g7d_le_one, SieveCurve7D.g7d_half)
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. For every real $L$, the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2}
holds with $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:curve-star}, $g^*$ is a density curve under the two inputs, with $g^* \le 1$ and $g^*(1/2) =
1$. Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen4} with the set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:leaf-zone}.
\end{proof}

The sieve meets the leaves as sums of $S(\mathcal A_d, w)$ over tuples of primes. The next lemma is
Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two} for them, with the sifting at $w$ in place of the last prime and the $w$-rough cofactor in the
second variable.

\begin{lemma}[a leaf in two variables]\label{lem:leaf-term}
% Lean: SieveLeafTwo.leavesE_of_zone, SieveLeafTwo.LeavesE, SieveLeafTwo.leaf2_bound
Let $0 < L \le 5/2$ and let $\mathcal R$ be a set of pairs for which the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds at
$L$ with $\mathcal R$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$. Fix $e_0 > 0$, integers $K_0, J_0 \ge 0$, and $\Lambda_1 \ge 0$. There
are $\delta > 0$ and $q_0$ such that the following holds for every $q \ge q_0$, every $a$ prime to $q$ and every integer
$w \ge 2$ with $w \ge q^{e_0}$. Let $P_1, P_1', R_-, R_+$ be integers with $P_1, P_1', R_+ \ge 1$, $R_- \ge 0$ and $P_1'
R_+ \le N_1$, such that $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R$ for every $m_1 \in [\log_q P_1, \log_q P_1']$ and every $m_2$ with
$\lfloor N_1/R_+ \rfloor \le 4q^{m_1 + m_2}$ and $q^{m_1 + m_2} \le \lfloor N_2/(R_- + 1) \rfloor$. Let $K \le K_0$; let
$\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S_r$ be disjoint subsets of $\{1, \dots, K\}$ with $\mathcal S_r \ne \emptyset$; let $(y_1,
\dots, y_K)$ run over the $K$-tuples of primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy at most $J_0$ comparisons with constant terms at
most $\Lambda_1 \log q$; and suppose that every tuple of primes that satisfies them has $y_i \ge w$ for every $i$, $P_1
< \prod_{\mathcal S} y_i \le P_1'$ and $R_- < \prod_{\mathcal S_r} y_i \le R_+$. Then, with $d = y_1 \cdots y_K$,
\[
  \Bigl|\sum_{(y_1, \dots, y_K)} \Bigl(S(\mathcal A_d, w) - \frac{S(\mathcal B_d, w)}{\varphi(q)}\Bigr)\Bigr|
  \le \frac{q^{L - \delta}}{\varphi(q)}.
\]
\end{lemma}

The first variable is $\prod_{\mathcal S} y_i$, the rest $\prod_{\mathcal S_r} y_i$, and the second variable the
integer divided by the two: the cofactor $n/d$ and the primes of the tuple off $\mathcal S \cup \mathcal S_r$. The
tuples need neither be ordered nor have distinct entries.

\begin{proof}
For one $K$ the proof is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}. Every prime of $d$ is at least $w$, so an integer $n$ with $d
\mid n$ has no prime factor below $w$ exactly when its cofactor $m = n/d$ has none. Such an $m \le N_2 < q^{L + 1}$ is
the product of a unique non-increasing tuple $z_1 \ge \dots \ge z_t \ge w$ of primes with $t \le t_1 = \lceil (L +
1)/e_0 \rceil$, since $w \ge q^{e_0}$. For each $t$, the pairs $(\mathbf y, \mathbf z)$ are the $(K + t)$-tuples of
primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy the given comparisons on $\mathbf y$, the comparisons $z_b \le z_a$ for $a \le b$ and
$z_a \ge w$, and $N_1 < \prod \mathbf y \prod \mathbf z \le N_2$: at most $J_0 + t_1^2 + t_1 + 2$ comparisons, with
constant terms at most $(\Lambda_1 + L + 1)\log q$. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:chains-two} to them with $\mathcal S_1 =
\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S_2$ the indices off $\mathcal S \cup \mathcal S_r$, among them all those of $\mathbf z$, with
$P_1$ and $P_1'$ those of the lemma, $P_2 = \lfloor N_1/(P_1'R_+) \rfloor \ge 1$, $P_2' = \lfloor N_2/((P_1 + 1)(R_- +
1)) \rfloor$ and the
band $M_{\mathrm{lo}} = \lfloor N_1/R_+ \rfloor \ge 1$, $M_{\mathrm{hi}} = \lfloor N_2/(R_- + 1) \rfloor$. The region's
hypothesis is the one assumed. There are at most $t_1 + 1$ values of $t$, and half the least of their $\delta$'s will do
for large $q$. For all $K \le K_0$ at once, take the least $\delta$ and the largest $q_0$ of the $K_0 + 1$ lengths.
\end{proof}

Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three} applies the lemma with $\mathcal R = \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}, to the third part of the leaves of a comparison.

\subsection{Harman's comparisons with the leaves cut in three}\label{sec:leaves-three}

Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill} evaluate the leaves of Harman's comparison by
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}, and their hypothesis (d) is that lemma's condition on the two blocks of a leaf: with $d_1$ the
product of the primes of $\mathcal S_1$ and, if $\iota = 1$, of the new primes, and $d_2$ the product of the others, a
leaf has $d_1 \le 4q^{D_1}$ and $d_2 \le 4q^{D_2}$, and (d) asks
\[
  D_1 < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1, 1) + D_1 + 2D_2 < 2L - 1.
\]
In outline: only the large leaves need it, those with $d = d_1 d_2 > q^{L - 1 - 4c'}$, for the small $c' > 0$ of the
proof, whose integers $n \le 2x$ have a cofactor $n/d$ below $2q^{1 + 4c'}$. For $D_1 \ge 1$ the second condition reads
$D_1 + D_2 < L - 1/2$: the leaves' products stay below about $x/\sqrt q$, and their cofactors above about $\sqrt q$. A
box whose leaves fail (d) fails the tests (J2) and (J3) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} with that choice of $\mathcal S_1$
and $\iota$, and its terms at a floor are not evaluated by them. For $D_1 \ge 1$ the large leaves that (d) leaves out
are those whose cofactor may be shorter than $\sqrt q$. For them the cofactor is a variable of its own, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} evaluates them where their exponents lie in the leaves' region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ of
Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}, with $d_1$ as the first variable, the cofactor as the second and $d_2$ as the rest; it
has no main term.

This subsection cuts each size of the large leaves in three, by two more comparisons, with exponents $D_1^*$ and
$D_2^*$ of $q$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(T1)] the leaves with $d_1 \le q^{D_1^*}$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} applies at the exponents $(D_1^*, D_2)$ in
  place of $(D_1, D_2)$;
\item[(T2)] those with $d_1 > q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 \le q^{D_2^*}$, where it applies at $(D_1, D_2^*)$;
\item[(T3)] those with $d_1 > q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 > q^{D_2^*}$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} applies, with $d_1$ in
  $(\lfloor q^{D_1^*} \rfloor, \lfloor 4q^{D_1} \rfloor]$ and $d_2$ in $(\lfloor q^{D_2^*} \rfloor, \lfloor 4q^{D_2}
  \rfloor]$.
\end{enumerate}
So (d) is replaced by three conditions: Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}'s at $(D_1^*, D_2)$ and at $(D_1, D_2^*)$, and the
region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ on the rectangle of the third part. The two propositions below are
Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill} with (d) so replaced, under the two inputs.

\begin{proposition}[Harman's comparison in two variables, the leaves cut in three]\label{prop:harman-two3}
% Lean: SieveTwoCompE.prop6_two3_at (SieveTwoCompE.prop6_two3); the region R^lf_L: SieveLeafTwo.LeafZoneX
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. Let $2 < L \le 5/2$ and let $\mathcal R$ be a region on which
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds at $L$, as in Section~\ref{sec:type1}. Fix integers $j \ge 1$ and $J_0$, $\Lambda_1 \ge
0$, an exponent $a_0 > 0$, exponents $l_i \le h_i$ for $1 \le i \le j$, disjoint sets $\mathcal S_0, \mathcal S_r
\subseteq \{1, \dots, j\}$ with $\mathcal S_r \ne \emptyset$, a set $\mathcal S_1 \subseteq \{1, \dots, j\}$, a side
$\iota \in \{1, 2\}$ and reals $t$, $u$, $v$, $\rho_1$, $\rho_2$, $\varepsilon > 0$ and $\eta > 0$ with (a), (b) and
(c) of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two}, and
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(d$_3$)] with $D_1 = \sum_{\mathcal S_1} h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 1]\, t$ and $D_2 = \sum_{i \notin \mathcal S_1}
  h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 2]\, t$: $D_1 \ge 0$, $D_2 \ge 0$, $D_1 + D_2 < L$, and there are reals $D_1^* \ge 0$, $D_2^*
  \ge 0$ and $\varepsilon_E > 0$ with
  \begin{gather*}
    D_1^* < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1^*, 1) + D_1^* + 2D_2 < 2L - 1, \\
    D_1 < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1, 1) + D_1 + 2D_2^* < 2L - 1,
  \end{gather*}
  and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ whenever $D_1^* - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 \le D_1 + \varepsilon_E$ and
  $L - D_2 - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 + m_2 \le L - D_2^* + \varepsilon_E$.
\end{enumerate}
Then the conclusion of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Harman's comparison with the rest among the tree's primes, the leaves cut in
three]\label{prop:harman-fill3}
% Lean: SieveFillCompE.prop6_fill3_at (SieveFillCompE.prop6_fill3)
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. Let $L$ and $\mathcal R$ be as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3}. Fix integers $j \ge 0$ and $J_0$, $\Lambda_1 \ge 0$, an exponent $a_0 > 0$,
exponents $l_i \le h_i$ for $1 \le i \le j$, a set $\mathcal S_0 \subseteq \{1, \dots, j\}$, a set $\mathcal S_1
\subseteq \{1, \dots, j\}$, a side $\iota \in \{1, 2\}$ and reals $t \ge 0$, $u$, $v$, $\tau$, $\rho_2$, $\varepsilon >
0$ and $\eta > 0$ with (a), (b) and (c) of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, and (d$_3$) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3} with
\[
  D_1 = \sum_{\mathcal S_1} h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 1](t + \rho_2), \qquad D_2 = \sum_{i \notin \mathcal S_1} h_i +
  \mathbf 1[\iota = 2](t + \rho_2).
\]
Then the conclusion of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} holds. For $j = 0$ the sum is the single term $S(\mathcal A,
z_0) - S(\mathcal B, z_0)/\varphi(q)$.
\end{proposition}

In (d$_3$) the first line is Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}'s condition for the first part, with $D_1^*$ in place of $D_1$, and
the second for the second part, with $D_2^*$ in place of $D_2$. In the region's condition $m_1$ is the exponent of
$d_1$, $m_2$ that of the cofactor and $L - m_1 - m_2$ that of $d_2$: the rectangle is that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} on the ranges of the third part, with the margin $\varepsilon_E$ for the integer parts and
the factors $4$. The condition $D_1 + D_2 < L$ makes the product of the two ranges' upper ends at most $x$, as
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} asks; (d) implies it, (d$_3$) has to say it. The two inputs enter through the estimate on
$\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}). When $D_1, D_2 \ge 0$, (d) implies (d$_3$): take $D_2^* =
0$, a small $\varepsilon_E$, and $D_1^*$ slightly above $D_1 + 2\varepsilon_E$, so that the rectangle of the third
part is empty. Under the two inputs, Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two3} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} therefore extend
Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill}.

\begin{proof}
The proof is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} (\S S3) with the large leaves of each size cut in three; \S S8
gives the details. The stops are as there, and Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two} evaluates them with the hypotheses (a), (b)
and (c). So do the tuples whose first variable is already in its range. The small leaves, $d \le q^{L - 1 - 4c'}$, are
counted by the bound of the leaves in \S S3. The large leaves of one size $s$ form a family of decreasing tuples of
primes in $[w, N_2]$, with $d_1 \le 4q^{D_1}$ and $d_2 \le 4q^{D_2}$. The two comparisons $d_1 \le q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2
\le q^{D_2^*}$ cut it into the three parts (T1), (T2) and (T3); each has a pivot when both sides of the leaf are
nonempty,
and when one side is empty, all the large leaves of the size lie in (T1) or in (T2).

On (T1), $d_1 \le q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 \le 4q^{D_2}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} applies with the exponents that the
proof
of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} takes for $(D_1, D_2)$, now taken for $(D_1^*, D_2)$: $\xi = L - 1 - \max(D_1^*, 1)
- 2c'$, $c = \xi - 20c'$, $\delta' = 4c'$ and $\kappa = 16c'$. On (T2), $d_1 \le 4q^{D_1}$ and $d_2 \le q^{D_2^*}$, and
it applies with the same exponents for $(D_1, D_2^*)$. Here $c' = \min(1, \omega_*)/32$, where $\omega_* > 0$ is the
least of the four margins $L - 1 - \max(D_1^*, 1)$, $2L - 1 - \max(D_1^*, 1) - D_1^* - 2D_2$, $L - 1 - \max(D_1, 1)$ and
$2L - 1 - \max(D_1, 1) - D_1 - 2D_2^*$ of (d$_3$). Each part leaves the bound
\[
  \frac{\zeta_k (N_2 - N_1)}{\varphi(q) \log w} \sum \frac1d + \frac{q^{L - \delta_3}}{\varphi(q)},
\]
with the sum of $1/d$ over the part. On (T3), Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term}, with $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ as its region
by Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}, $d_1$ as the first variable and $d_2$ as the rest, bounds the whole sum by $q^{L -
\delta_3}/\varphi(q)$: there is no main term, since Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} bounds the difference over $\mathcal A$
and $\mathcal B/\varphi(q)$ itself. Its hypotheses are (d$_3$): $D_1 + D_2 < L$ gives $\lfloor 4q^{D_1} \rfloor \lfloor
4q^{D_2} \rfloor \le N_1$ for large $q$, and the region's condition, with $q^{\varepsilon_E} \ge 64$, gives the region
on
the rectangle that the lemma asks for.

The sums of $1/d$ over (T1) and over (T2) add up to at most the sum over the whole size, since the parts are disjoint.
So the main terms of the size are at most those of the uncut size, and their total over the sizes is bounded as in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two}, by the same choice of the level $k$ of Brun's sieve. The error of a size is
$3q^{L - \delta_3}/\varphi(q)$ in place of $q^{L - \delta_3}/\varphi(q)$: one for (T1), one for (T2) and the whole bound
of
(T3). With at most $s_{\max}$ sizes the errors total $3s_{\max} q^{L - \delta_3}/\varphi(q)$, which changes only the
final $\delta$. For Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} the same cut is made at each size of the leaves of each tree of
the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, with the growth $t + \rho_2$ in $D_1$ and $D_2$; there are at most
$2s_{\max} + s_{\max}^2$ families of large leaves, and the errors total $3(2s_{\max} + s_{\max}^2)q^{L -
\delta_3}/\varphi(q)$.
\end{proof}

\emph{The tests.} The condition (d$_3$) becomes a condition on a box's exponents in units of $1/D$, as (d) became
(L). For integers $A, B \ge 0$, write (L) at $(A, B)$ for the leaves' condition (L) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} with
$a_1 = A/D$ and $a_2 = B/D$. The region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ is tested through nineteen half-planes that imply
it. For reals $m_1$, $m_2$ and $\rho$, let $(\mathrm Z)(m_1, m_2, \rho)$ be the cuts (C1) to (C7) of
Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}, with $\rho$ taken as a variable of its own in place of $L - m_1 - m_2$.

\begin{definition}\label{def:leaves3}
% Lean: SieveAd.leavesE, SieveAd.leavesL, SieveAd.pStar, SieveAd.thetaL, SieveLeafZoneX.leafXR, SieveLeafZoneX.LeafZoneXL
Let $A, B \ge 0$ be integers. The \emph{leaves cut in three} satisfy the condition $(\mathrm L_3)$ at $(A, B)$ if there
are integers $A^* \ge 1$ with (L) at $(A^*, B)$ and $B^* \ge 1$ with (L) at $(A, B^*)$, and, with $A^*$ and $B^*$ the
largest such,
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $A + B < D$;
\item[(ii)] each of the nineteen inequalities
  \begin{gather*}
    \tfrac9{10} \le m_1 \le \tfrac65, \qquad m_2 \ge \tfrac25, \qquad \tfrac3{10} \le \rho \le 1, \\
    (\mathrm Z)(m_1, m_2, \rho), \qquad (\mathrm Z)(m_1, \tfrac12, \rho),
  \end{gather*}
  with $m_2 = L - m_1 - \rho$, holds at the corner of the rectangle $[L(A^* - 1)/D, L(A + 1)/D] \times [L(B^* - 1)/D,
  L(B + 1)/D]$ of the pairs $(m_1, \rho)$ where it is tightest.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

So $A^*$ is the cut of $d_1$ and $B^*$ that of $d_2$, in units of $1/D$ of exponents of $x$, and the rectangle is
that of the third part, $d_1$ from about $x^{A^*/D}$ to $x^{A/D}$ and $d_2$ from about $x^{B^*/D}$ to $x^{B/D}$, with
one unit of margin on each side. Each of the nineteen inequalities is affine in $(m_1, \rho)$, so it holds on the whole
rectangle when it holds at that corner. On the rectangle they imply $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$. If
$m_2 \le 1/2$, the inequalities of $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ are among them and $m_2 \le 1/2$. If $m_2 \ge 1/2$,
those of $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L - m_2 + 1/2}$ at $(m_1, 1/2)$, with the same rest $\rho$, are among them but for
$2/5 \le 1/2 \le 1/2$, which hold, and this is the other half of Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}. The largest $A^*$ and
$B^*$ are explicit: (L) at $(A^*, B)$ is
three
upper bounds for $A^*$, and (L) at $(A, B^*)$ is a condition on $A$ and two upper bounds for $B^*$. For rational $L$
every condition is an inequality between integers, and the formalisation checks it so, with the integer parts of the
bounds for $A^*$ and $B^*$.
% Lean: SieveLeafZoneX.leafXR_sound, SieveLeafZoneX.leafZoneX_of_XL, SieveAd.leavesE_cuts, SieveAd.leavesL_real

Among the tests of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}, besides those of the window (Section~\ref{sec:window-tests}), are those of
Definition~\ref{def:tests} and of Section~\ref{sec:reversal} with the leaves' condition relaxed. Say that a box passes
(J2) or (J3) \emph{with (L) or (L$_3$)} at a floor $f$ if it passes the test of Definition~\ref{def:tests} at $f$ with
the condition ``(L) holds, or (L$_3$) holds at $(A, B)$'' in place of ``(L) holds'', where $A = h(\mathcal S_1) +
\mathbf 1[\iota = 1]\, g$ and $B = h(\mathfrak b) - h(\mathcal S_1) + \mathbf 1[\iota = 2]\, g$ are the numerators of
$a_1$ and $a_2$, with the growth $g$ of the test. Over a frozen part, (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) and (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) with
(L) or (L$_3$) are read in the same way, with $h(\mathcal S_1)$ and $h(\mathfrak b) - h(\mathcal S_1)$ replaced by
$\overline e(\mathcal S_1) + \gamma$ and $\overline e(\overline{\mathcal S_1}) + \gamma$, with the room $\gamma$ of
Section~\ref{sec:reversal}. Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} keeps
the tests of Definition~\ref{def:tests} with (L) alone; the relaxed tests enter only the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:tests3}
% Lean: SieveAd.fillTests_sound, SieveAd.evJ_twoE, SieveAd.evJ_fillE, SieveAd.evJ_two_core3, SieveAd.evJ_fill_core3,
% SieveAd.evJ_two_core, SieveAd.evJ_fill_core; over a frozen part: SieveAd.frozTests_sound, SieveAd.evJ_fam_two,
% SieveAd.evJ_fam_fill; the tests: SieveAd.chkJ2, SieveAd.chkJ3, SieveAd.okJ2, SieveAd.okJ3, SieveAd.fillTests,
% SieveAd.coreJ2, SieveAd.coreJ3, SieveAd.okJF2, SieveAd.okJF3, SieveAd.frozTests
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] The terms at the floor $f$ of a box that passes (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$) at $f$ are evaluated.
\item[(b)] For every $J$, (a) holds uniformly when the family is cut by at most $J$ further comparisons of the kinds of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c): $q_0$ does not depend on them. This holds also for boxes of more than $13$ primes that
  satisfy the conditions of (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$).
\item[(c)] Let $\mathcal M$ be the wide part of a box of $k \ge 2$ primes with $0 < l_1 \le h_1 \le D$. If a chain
  $\mathfrak c$ of $j \ge 0$ primes passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) with (L) or (L$_3$) at $f$, then
  $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c; f) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Where (L) holds, these are Lemmas~\ref{lem:tests}(b), (c) and~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}. Let (L$_3$) hold at $(A, B)$, with
$A^*$ and $B^*$ its cuts. (a) For (J2), apply Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3} with the region $\mathcal R$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} and all the choices of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b), and with $D_1^* = LA^*/D$, $D_2^*
= LB^*/D$ and $\varepsilon_E = L/(2D)$. Hypotheses (a), (b) and (c) were checked there. For (d$_3$), $D_1 = LA/D$ and
$D_2 = LB/D$ are nonnegative, and $D_1 + D_2 < L$ by (i). The first line of (d$_3$) is (L) at $(A^*, B)$, multiplied by
$L$: its first inequality is $D_1^* < L - 1$, and $\max(D_1^*, 1) + D_1^* + 2D_2 < 2L - 1$ is its second if $D_1^* \ge
1$
and its third if $D_1^* \le 1$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b); the second line is (L) at $(A, B^*)$ in the
same way. For the region, let $D_1^* - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 \le D_1 + \varepsilon_E$ and $L - D_2 - \varepsilon_E \le
m_1 + m_2 \le L - D_2^* + \varepsilon_E$. Then $m_1 \in [L(A^* - 1)/D, L(A + 1)/D]$ and $\rho = L - m_1 - m_2 \in [L(B^*
- 1)/D, L(B + 1)/D]$, so by (ii) the nineteen inequalities hold at $(m_1, \rho)$, and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, as said after Definition~\ref{def:leaves3}. The proposition's bound is at most $\eta X$ for large
$q$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b). For (J3), apply Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} in the same way,
with $\tau = Lr_1/D$ and $\rho_2 = Lr_2/D$; its $D_1$ and $D_2$, with the growth $t + \rho_2 = Lg/D$, are again $LA/D$
and $LB/D$.

(b) The propositions hold for every family of comparisons with the stated bounds, with $\delta$ and $q_0$ that do not
depend on them, and for chains of any length; the argument is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c).

(c) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} writes $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c; f)$, up to $o(X)$, as a sum of
finitely many terms at the floor $f$ of boxes of cells of one unit, under the family's comparisons, and evaluates each
by Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} or~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, with the box's sums bounded by the frozen part's
exponents widened by the room $\gamma$. Where (L$_3$) holds, Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3}
or~\ref{prop:harman-fill3}
evaluates each instead, with the cuts $A^*$ and $B^*$ of (L$_3$) at those bounds: the box's $D_1$ and $D_2$ are at most
$LA/D$ and $LB/D$, and the conditions of (d$_3$) only weaken as $D_1$ and $D_2$ decrease, since the cuts $A^*$ and
$B^*$ are kept and the rectangle of the region shrinks into the rectangle of (ii). By (b) this holds uniformly in the
family's comparisons, and the sum over the finitely many boxes is $o(X)$.
\end{proof}

The condition ``(L) or (L$_3$)'' is weaker than (L), so a box that passes a test of Definition~\ref{def:tests} passes
it with (L) or (L$_3$). Roughly, in exponents of $x$, with $a_1^* = A^*/D$, $a_2^* = B^*/D$, $a_1 = A/D$ and $a_2 =
B/D$, and up to the margins, (L$_3$) asks (L) at $(a_1^*, a_2)$ and at $(a_1, a_2^*)$, $a_1 + a_2 < 1$, and the leaves'
region for $d_1$ from $x^{a_1^*}$ to $x^{a_1}$ and $d_2$ from $x^{a_2^*}$ to $x^{a_2}$. The cuts lie just inside (L),
and
the region's first five inequalities ask $d_1$ to lie between about $q^{9/10}$ and $q^{6/5}$, $d_2$ between about
$q^{3/10}$ and $q$, and the cofactor to be at least about $q^{2/5}$.

\subsection{The reversal of a term of the sign \texorpdfstring{$+$}{+}}\label{sec:reversal-plus}

A term with the sign $+$ needs a lower bound; role reversal is used for such terms, which would otherwise be discarded,
in the sieves of \cite{bhp} and \cite{li-harman}. A box of the sign $+$ that no test evaluates is dropped at its full
share
$c_D(\mathfrak b)/\mathcal K$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:drop}(1)), or decomposed by Buchstab's identity into a term at a floor and
boxes of the sign $-$, which need upper bounds. The reversal of Section~\ref{sec:reversal} applies to it with the roles
of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ exchanged: the lower inequality of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich} goes to $\mathcal A$ and
the upper one to $\mathcal B$. The term is then bounded from below by the terms at the level $\nu$ over the narrow part
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, less the ends, and Buchstab's identity between the floor and the level asks for an upper
bound of the chain $((f, \nu))$ over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$: a tree with the sign $-$.

\emph{Frozen parts other than the wide one.} Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} are stated for the wide part. Their proofs use of the frozen part only its ends $e_\pm$, in
the tests, and the range $R \in (x^{(D - h_1)/D}, x^{(D - l_1 + 1)/D}]$, in the nodes (V$_{\mathcal M}$) and
(D$_{\mathcal M}$) and the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree}. They hold, with the same proofs, for the narrow part
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm n} = \mathcal M(D - h_1 + 1, D - l_1)$, which lies in that range: the tests (I$_{\mathcal M}$),
(J2$_{\mathcal M}$) and (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) are read with $(e_-, e_+) = (D - h_1 + 1, D - l_1)$, and (V$_{\mathcal M}$)
and (D$_{\mathcal M}$) keep their conditions and costs, in terms of $l_1$ and $h_1$. The formalisation proves
Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} for every frozen part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_- \le e_+ \le D$, with the same room,
and the nodes of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} for every family of pairs whose $R$ lie in that range; for the narrow part
these are the statements used below. A tree over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ is meant in this sense; its cost stands for
its multiple of $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n})/\mathcal K$. The definition of $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ is read
with $e_- \le e_+$: when $h_1 = l_1 + 1$ the narrow part is $\mathcal M(D - l_1, D - l_1)$, which is empty, with its
terms and weight $0$.
% Lean: SieveAd.frozTests_sound, SieveAd.frozRules, SieveAd.inSlab_frozS_inner, SieveAd.InSlab

\begin{definition}\label{def:reversal-plus}
% Lean: SieveAd.flipOK, SieveAd.flipT, SieveAd.flipTreeD, SieveAd.flipCostB, SieveAd.flipTreeDYH, SieveAd.flipCostBYH, SieveAd.flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH, SieveAd.bdK
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box that may be reversed (Definition~\ref{def:reversal}), and $\nu = \lceil h_1/2 \rceil$. A
\emph{reversal of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign $+$} is a floor $f$ with $0 < f \le \min(l_k, \nu)$ at which the chain of
no prime passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) over the narrow part $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, with the room
of Section~\ref{sec:reversal}, and a tree over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ on the chain $((f, \nu))$ with the sign
$-$, whose tests are read over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$; at $21/10$ its nodes (E$_{\mathcal M}$) may also use the
tests (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests} over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$. If
that tree's cost is $c_F$, the reversal's cost is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cRplus}
  c_R^+ = \Bigl\lceil \frac{c_F\, h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + c_e E}{D\mathcal K} \Bigr\rceil,
\end{equation}
with $E$, $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}}$, $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$ and $c_e$ as in (\ref{eq:cR}).
\end{definition}

The conditions on the box are those of a reversal with the sign $-$, and the formalisation checks them by one test for
both signs. The difference from (\ref{eq:cR}) is that $c_F$ multiplies $h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b)$ alone, not $h_1
c_D(\mathfrak b) + E$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:reversal-plus}
% Lean: SieveAd.bd_flip_pos, SieveAd.bd_flip_core_pos, SieveAd.bd_flip_coreB, SieveAd.bd_flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH_sound, SieveAd.froz_top_ge, SieveAd.froz_ends_le, SieveAd.dJF_level, SieveAd.sum_frozS_three, SieveAd.bd_of_eps, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTY
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 2$ primes and $\nu = \lceil h_1/2 \rceil$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Let $0 < l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\nu \le l_1$, $h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$ and $h_2 \le l_1$. Let $0 \le f \le \nu$ be
  an integer, $C \ge 0$, $C_e \ge \Omega[(l_1 - 1)/(\nu + 1), (h_1 + 2)/\nu)\, D/(\nu\mathcal K)$, $w_0$ real and $w_1,
  w_2 \ge 0$. Suppose that $\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; f) = o(X)$; that for every $\eta > 0$, for large
  $q$ and every $a$ prime to $q$, $-\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(((f, \nu))) \ge -(C \operatorname{wt}(\mathcal
  M_{\mathrm n}) + \eta) X$; and that for every $\delta > 0$ and large $q$, $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n})
  \le w_0 + \delta$, $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M(D - h_1, D - h_1 + 1)) \le w_1 + \delta$ and
  $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M(D - l_1, D - l_1 + 1)) \le w_2 + \delta$. Then the term of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign
  $+$ is bounded at $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2)$.
\item[(b)] If $\mathfrak b$ may be reversed, with a reversal with the sign $+$ of cost $c_R^+$ whose tree passes, then
  the term of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign $+$ is bounded at $c_R^+/\mathcal K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

The proof of (a) is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal} with the inequalities of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}
exchanged between the two sets; (b) checks the hypotheses of (a) from the reversal.

\begin{proof}
(a) Since $h_1 > 0$, $\nu > 0$ and $h_1 \le 2\nu$. Write $\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}$ for the union of the two ends. Since
$l_1 < h_1$, $D - h_1 + 1 \le D - l_1$, and the wide part is the disjoint union of the lower end, the narrow part and
the upper end. By Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}, applied to $\mathcal A$ for the lower inequality, which uses $\nu \le l_1$,
$h_1 \le 2\nu$ and $h_2 \le l_1$, and to $\mathcal B$ for the upper,
\[
  \Delta(\mathfrak b) \ge T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\mathcal A, \emptyset; \nu) - \frac{T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm
  w}}(\mathcal B, \emptyset; \nu)}{\varphi(q)} = \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; \nu) - \frac{T_{\mathcal
  M_{\mathrm e}}(\mathcal B, \emptyset; \nu)}{\varphi(q)}.
\]
By (\ref{eq:levels}) over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, with $f \le \nu \le l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm
n}}(\emptyset; \nu) = \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; f) - \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(((f, \nu)))$. The
first term is at least $-\eta X$ for large $q$, and by the hypothesis on the chain the second is at least $-(C
\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) + \eta) X$. The ends lie in the wide range, so, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal}, for $R$ in them $y = x/R \in [x^{(l_1 - 1)/D}, x^{h_1/D})$, the integers of $\mathcal
B$ counted for $R$ at the level $\nu$ are at most $\Phi(y, z_\nu)$, the arguments $\log 2y/\log z_\nu$ lie in $[(l_1 -
1)/(\nu + 1), (h_1 + 2)/\nu)$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:rough} gives $T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}}(\mathcal B, \emptyset;
\nu)/\varphi(q) \le (C_e + \eta) \operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}) X$ for large $q$; the formalisation uses
$h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$ there. The hypotheses on the weights give $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) \le w_0 +
\eta$ and $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}) \le w_1 + w_2 + 2\eta$. As $C \ge 0$, collecting gives
$\Delta(\mathfrak b) \ge -(C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) + O(\eta)) X$, with an $O$ that depends only on $C$, $C_e$ and the
$w_i$.

(b) Let $C = c_F/\mathcal K$, $C_e = c_e/\mathcal K$, $w_0 = (h_1/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b)/\mathcal K$, $w_1 = ((h_1 +
1)/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}})/\mathcal K$ and $w_2 = ((l_1 + 1)/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}})/\mathcal
K$, so that $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) = (c_F h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + c_e E)/(D\mathcal K^2) \le c_R^+/\mathcal K$. A box
that may be reversed has $0 < l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\nu \le l_1$, $h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$ and $h_2 \le l_1 - 1$, and $C_e$
satisfies the condition of (a) by the definition of $c_e$. Every prime of $R$ is at least $a_k \ge x^{l_k/D} \ge
x^{f/D}$, and the chain of no prime passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) at $f$ over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm
n}$; so $\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; f) = o(X)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} for $\mathcal
M_{\mathrm n}$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} for $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, with the sign $-$, and with the nodes
(E$_{\mathcal M}$) that pass (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ evaluated by
Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(b) for $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, for every $\eta' > 0$
the chain satisfies the hypothesis of (a) with $C + \eta'$ in place of $C$. The ends are the narrow parts of $\mathfrak
b^{\mathrm{lo}}$ and $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-weight} applies to $\mathfrak b$, as $h_1
\le 2\nu \le 2l_1$, and to $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}}$ and $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$, exactly as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal}; it gives the bounds of the weights with $w_0$, $w_1$, $w_2$. So (a) bounds the term at
$(C + \eta') w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2)$ for every $\eta' > 0$, and, as $w_0 \ge 0$, at $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) \le
c_R^+/\mathcal K$.
\end{proof}

\emph{Why the tree's cost weighs on the box's share alone.} For the sign $-$ the tree runs over the wide part, which is
the narrow part and the ends, and its cost multiplies $w_0 + w_1 + w_2$. For the sign $+$ the tree runs over the narrow
part only, from the side of $\mathcal A$; the ends come in only from the side of $\mathcal B$, through the upper
inequality of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}, and cost $C_e$ per unit of weight, by Lemma~\ref{lem:rough}. So $c_F$
multiplies $w_0$ alone, which is $h_1/D$ times the box's share. The price is that the chain $((f, \nu))$ has the sign
$-$: (D$_{\mathcal M}$) asks the sign $+$, so no cell of the chain's prime from $f$ to $\nu$ is dropped. Each is
empty, evaluated, cut, or decomposed by Buchstab's identity, whose chains of two primes have the sign $+$ and may be
dropped. This is a remark on Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree}, not a separate statement.

\emph{Which boxes the search reverses.} The search at $21/10$ (Section~\ref{sec:certificate-b}) reverses boxes of either
sign, of at most six primes: the bound $11$ on $k$ of Definition~\ref{def:reversal} is lowered to $6$ there.
In its main tree the root is one prime with the sign $-$, a node (B) adds one prime and
changes the sign, and a node (S) keeps both; so a box of $k$ primes has the sign $(-1)^k$, and the reversed boxes of
the sign $-$ have three or five primes, those of the sign $+$ two, four or six. This is a property of the search's plan,
not a theorem: the soundness of the reversal holds for either sign. Reversals are not nested:
a tree over a frozen part has no node (R). A tree over a frozen part is the same kind of tree for both signs, with the
same nodes and the same tests read over its part. Only three things change with the sign of the reversed box: the part,
wide for $-$ and narrow for $+$; the sign of the chain $((f, \nu))$, $+$ for $-$ and $-$ for $+$; and the factor of
$c_F$ in the cost, $h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + E$ in (\ref{eq:cR}) and $h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b)$ in (\ref{eq:cRplus}).

\section{The certificate at \texorpdfstring{$21/10$}{21/10}, and the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}}\label{sec:certificate-b}

This section puts the rules of Sections~\ref{sec:window} and~\ref{sec:rules} into the trees of
Section~\ref{sec:decomp}, describes the search that Lean's kernel follows at $L = 21/10$ and its result, and proves
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}. The notation of Sections~\ref{sec:decomp} and~\ref{sec:certificate} is kept.

\subsection{The trees of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}}

\begin{definition}\label{def:tree-b}
% Lean: SieveAd.Rules, SieveAd.opsMP, SieveAd.resTestsFYH, SieveAd.fillTests, SieveAd.leavesE; the reversal of either sign: SieveAd.flipOKP, SieveAd.flipTreeDYH, SieveAd.flipCostBYH, SieveAd.flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH; over a frozen part: SieveAd.opsKP, SieveAd.frozResTestsYH
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 1$ primes and $\varepsilon$ a sign. A \emph{tree of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}} on
$\mathfrak b$ with the sign $\varepsilon$ is a tree of Definition~\ref{def:tree}, with trees over frozen parts of
Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} in its reversals, with three changes.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] A node (E) passes if $\mathfrak b$ passes (I), or (W) or (W$'$) of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}, with
  or without the cofactor. A node (E$_{\mathcal M}$) of a tree over a frozen part passes if its chain passes
  (I$_{\mathcal M}$), or (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$), with or without the chain's cofactor. Cost $0$.
\item[(ii)] The tests (J2) and (J3) of a node (B), and (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) and (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) at the floor of
  a reversal, are taken with (L) or (L$_3$) (Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three}).
\item[(iii)] A new node (R$^+$), \emph{reversed with the sign $+$}: $\varepsilon = +$, and a reversal of $\mathfrak b$
  with the sign $+$ (Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus}). Cost $c_R^+$ of (\ref{eq:cRplus}).
\end{enumerate}
The trees over the frozen parts of the reversals of either sign, over the wide part for (R) and over the narrow part
for (R$^+$), have the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} with the changes (i) and (ii).
\end{definition}

The cost of such a tree is, as before, the sum of the costs of its dropped boxes and of its reversals: the boxes
evaluated by the window cost nothing, like those evaluated by (I). A box of the sign $+$ may now be dropped, decomposed,
cut, evaluated or reversed, and so may a box of the sign $-$ but for the drop. A tree over a frozen part has no node
(R) or (R$^+$): the reversals are not nested.

Under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} holds for the trees of
Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}, with its proof and three more
cases: a node (E) by (W) or (W$'$) is Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(a); a node (B) whose test passes with (L$_3$) uses
Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) in place of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b); and a node (R$^+$) is
Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b). Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) holds, with the same proof, for every frozen part
$\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_- \le e_+ \le D$, in particular the narrow part; it replaces Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}
at the floor of a reversal of either sign, in Propositions~\ref{prop:reversal} and~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b), and at
the nodes (B) of the trees over the frozen parts. Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} holds for their trees over the frozen
parts, the wide and the narrow ones, with Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(b) for the nodes (E$_{\mathcal M}$) and
Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) for the tests at a floor. And
Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} holds with the root's test (J3) taken with (L) or (L$_3$), by Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) in place
of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b). In the formalisation the trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b} are not a type: their
leaves in the main tree, the nodes (V), (E) and the drops, are one-node trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree}, with the
tests of the
search, bounded by Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak sound}, through
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak pr\_of\_has}); their other nodes are the rules of the search, which the kernel follows, and
the induction is that of the search (Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b}). The rules are
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak rulesMP} with the soundness of the tests, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak resTestsFYH\_sound}, for
the nodes other than the reversals; \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak bd\_flipCostDYH} for the reversals of either sign; and
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak rules\_frozResYH} for the nodes over a frozen part. Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} is
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak linnik\_pr}, which takes any sound tests: at $21/10$ the tests (I), (J2) and (J3) with (L) or
(L$_3$), sound by \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak fillTests\_sound}.

\subsection{The search and its soundness}

The search is that of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}, led by hints, with the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}. Its
tests are the conditions of Definitions~\ref{def:tests} and~\ref{def:frozen-tree} with (L) or (L$_3$), in integers, and
the kernel's tests of (W) and (W$'$) of Section~\ref{sec:window-tests}, led by the records that the hints give; its
costs are (\ref{eq:cD}), $c'_D$, (\ref{eq:cR}) and (\ref{eq:cRplus}). A reversal of either sign is searched as in
Section~\ref{sec:certificate}: the test at its floor over the wide or the narrow part, then the tree over that part on
$((f, \nu))$, with the sign opposite to the box's, searched with the fixed limit $64\mathcal K$. Its soundness is proved
once, for every hint.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:search-b}
% Lean: SieveAd.pr_of_partDYH, SieveAd.solveMDYH_sound, SieveAd.solveD_sound, SieveAd.stepD_sound, SieveAd.rulesMP, SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.altFDYH_sound, SieveAd.bd_flipCostDYH, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.kidsM_sound, SieveAd.kidsF_sound, SieveAd.kidsC_sound, SieveAd.partD_sound, SieveAd.pr_split, SieveAd.pr_mono; at 21/10: Linnik21000.part21000, Linnik21000.soundF21000
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, and let $2 < L \le 5/2$ be rational. Whatever the hints, the way
the configurations of the main tree are read from them, the grids of the cells of the new primes, the depths of
recursion of both searches and the bound, at most $11$ (Definition~\ref{def:reversal}), on the number of primes of a
reversed box:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] if the search with the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b} returns on a box of $k \ge 1$ primes, a sign and
a
  limit a value $c$ below the limit, then the term of the box with that sign is bounded at $c/\mathcal K$;
\item[(b)] let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 0$ primes, $\varepsilon$ a sign and $\alpha_0 < \alpha_1 < \dots <
  \alpha_N$ integers. If the values of the search on the boxes $\mathfrak b \oplus (\alpha_{i - 1}, \alpha_i)$,
  $1 \le i \le N$, with the sign $\varepsilon$, the hints read in turn and each with the limit $\mathcal K$ less
  the values before it, are each below its limit and add up to at most $c < \mathcal K$, then the term of $\mathfrak b
  \oplus (\alpha_0, \alpha_N)$ with the sign $\varepsilon$ is bounded at $c/\mathcal K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) is the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:search}, by induction on the depth of recursion, with the cases of
Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}. A value below the limit comes from a node whose condition the search has evaluated to true.
An evaluation whose kernel's test of (W) or (W$'$) passes, with or without the cofactor, is a node (E), by
Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c); one whose test (I) passes is a node (E) as before. A step of Buchstab's identity whose
test passes is a node (B), its test being (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$), and Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) evaluates its
terms at the floor; the values of the cells of the new prime add up by cuts (S), since any point strictly inside a range
is a cut. A reversal of either sign has passed the conditions of Definition~\ref{def:reversal} on the box, with at most
the search's bound of primes, and the test at its floor over the wide part for the sign $-$ and over the narrow part for
the sign $+$
(Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus}); its tree over that part has, by the same induction with the nodes over that part
(Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c) for the window's tests, Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) for the tests at a floor), a value
below $64\mathcal K$, which is the cost $c_F$ of a tree that passes; and Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal}
or~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b) bounds the term at the reversal's cost. A branch whose condition fails returns its limit,
which says nothing. (b) follows from (a) by induction on $N$: each value is below its limit, so (a) bounds each cell's
term, and Lemma~\ref{lem:box-identities}(a) adds the bounds.
\end{proof}

So the hints are not trusted at $21/10$ either: a wrong hint can make the check fail, not make it wrong.

\subsection{The plan at \texorpdfstring{$21/10$}{21/10}}

The grid of the new primes' cells, which the search in the Lean fixes (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak solve21000},
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak stepOf}), has cells of side at most a tenth of their lower end and at most $3298534883/D$,
about $0.003$,
for the first four primes of a box of the main tree, twice that for the fifth and sixth and three times for the seventh
and eighth; over a frozen part $3298534883/D$ for the chain's first two primes and twice that from the third (at
$43/20$, $4398046511/D$ in the main tree and $6597069767/D$ over the frozen parts). The strategy at $21/10$ differs from
that of Section~\ref{sec:certificate} in the following; as there, it needs no proof, and its choices affect only the
cost. The depth is $8$: boxes of fewer than eight primes are decomposed. Boxes of two to six primes are reversed, of
either sign (Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus}).
Over a frozen part, chains of fewer than three primes are decomposed, as before, and the plan tries every configuration
of (I$_{\mathcal M}$) that the hints can name, not the strategy's proposal alone, and for a step of Buchstab's
identity it takes as its floor the greatest of the sixteen floors that the four bits of the hint name, at most the
box's bound, at which the sets that the hint names pass. Besides (I), the strategy proposes the
tests of the resonant window: (W) for a rest of the box's primes, with the cofactor when the box's cells leave room for
one prime, and (W$'$)
where no rest passes (W). Consecutive cells that one test of the window evaluates are one cell. A box is cut in two at
the middle of its newest cell, up to three times, where the top of that cell passes the window, and a box of two primes
of the main tree is cut in two at the middle of its newest cell, down to half the grid's step, where that lowers its
cost, the kernel's time priced as follows. Each step is chosen by its cost plus $0.3 \cdot
10^{-6}\mathcal K$ for each second of the kernel's time that the generator's model predicts for it.
% Lean (the search at 21/10; the strategy is tools/linnik/hint_cert.py, CERTS['21000']): SieveAd.solve21000, SieveAd.solveD, SieveAd.stepD, SieveAd.kidsM, SieveAd.kidsF, SieveAd.kidsC, SieveAd.kidsCS, SieveAd.stepOf, SieveAd.cfgIMYH, SieveAd.cfgIFYH, SieveAd.cfgJM, SieveAd.cfgJF, SieveAd.altFDYH, SieveAd.partD

The hints are those of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}, with two changes. The code of an evaluation has one more bit,
which chooses between (I) and the window. And in the main tree the sets of an evaluation have one more bit, the last,
for the cofactor, as the sets over a frozen part already had at $43/20$. The codes of a box are:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lp{0.72\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Hints & What the search does at the box \\
\midrule
\texttt{00} & evaluates the box by (I): the rest's cell, $0$ for the first or $1$ and three bits for its index, then the
set
  $\mathcal S$ in $k + 1$ bits \\
\texttt{01} & evaluates the box by the window: the rest $\mathcal S_r$ in $k + 1$ bits, then four bits $n_1 \ne 0$
  and $n_1$ records of $15 + 2K$ bits, for (W); or \texttt{0000}, four bits $n_2 \ne 0$ and $n_2$ records
  of $15 + 3K$ bits, for (W) with the large values of the powers; or eight zeros, four bits each for $n_r$,
  $k_{\mathrm H}$ and $k_{\mathrm L}$, and $(15 + 3n_r)(k_{\mathrm H} + k_{\mathrm L})$ bits, for (W$'$) \\
\texttt{10} & Buchstab's identity, as at $43/20$ \\
\texttt{110} & drops the box \\
\texttt{1110} & reverses the box, of either sign: the floor and the configuration of the test over the wide or the
  narrow part, then the cuts of the range of the chain's first prime \\
\texttt{11110} & cuts the newest cell in two at its middle \\
\texttt{111110} & finds the box empty, (V) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Here $k$ is the number of primes of the box, and over a frozen part the number of the chain's and the frozen primes.
A record of (W) is an interval's end in thirteen bits, the line of the variable's term in two bits, and the line
of each of the rest's $K$ members (Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}, the cofactor included when it is in the rest)
in two bits, three with the large values of the powers; those of (W$'$) are those of its
$k_{\mathrm H}$ intervals at the upper corner and its $k_{\mathrm L}$ at the lower, with $n_r$ members; \S S7 has what
the lines are. The set $\mathcal S$ of (I) has the cofactor's bit, which (I) does not read. The configurations at a
floor and the codes of the cuts are those of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:certificate-b}
